Summary

the face behind the ronin suit was supposed to be some guy with a cruel face that wanted to kill. instead it was some girl that looked at him like he was her hero. he forgot how to be a hero, that died with natasha. but whatever had died in him with natasha, this little girl was reviving a part of him.

TLDR: I’m writing Hawkeye, and obviously, since this is before episode three and four, I might not predict everything correctly. Sorry in advance, but I am going to write beyond episode 2, so I apologize for what I don’t predict correctly.

    “Yeah?”

One night; this night, in Central Park, almost a year ago. And she was alone here, at night, dressed in some stupid uniform… and she couldn’t find her bow. She couldn’t find her weapons. She didn’t have one single arrow. She was defenseless, in Central Park. Kate wanted to scream out for anyone, but she was strangled with silence. It passed through her veins like a plague. Kate’s breath shuddered, and her fingers began to tremble, and she felt a panic attack that was rising over her.

    It was ripping through her spine, and she looked around, suddenly incredibly aware of everything around her. There was no noise around her. It was just a ringing in her ears, and she crossed her arms across her chest like she was freezing… like the air was acidic, burning her with every second that she breathed it in. Her breath was shaky, and she started to panic. Her eyes looked around, and that was when the noise started. There was this yelling in her ears, and she spun around, and she thought that it was right beside her. There was this cold, and she thought that it was the night’s breeze, and it was freezing that night, but it wasn’t windy. Kate spun around, and she whimpered, and she felt like a kid. She felt like a little girl; she felt like she was so vulnerable, and she freaking hated it. But the breathing came again, and she jumped, and she felt this chill run over her skin. It felt like his breath running over her skin, and there was suddenly a stinging in her eyes, and she felt his breath again, and she almost screamed. It felt like he was right there. And then she saw someone whisper her name in her ear, and she screamed, and she looked behind her, but there was nothing there. And then there was his face, and she gasped, and she jumped back, and her foot caught on a rock behind her and she fell back onto the grass, her hands scraping on the pebbles. But she didn’t even feel it.

    His eyes were staring down at her, and her breath was rapid, and she looked up to him, a few tears rolling down her cheeks. She knew he wasn’t real. He couldn’t be: she knew that it wasn’t real, but she was still terrified. All of that combat training, and all of that archery that she had done to protect herself from it happening again was all a joke. She was a joke. She was just a scared little girl again, and Kate looked up to him, her breathing cycling rapidly in and out of her. She was having an anxiety attack.

    This isn’t real. This isn’t real. She told herself that over and over again, and it just made her more scared. She was sure that she was losing her mind. And while she was telling herself that it wasn’t real, the vision of that man suddenly jumped atop her, and she could feel the pressure on her throat as he tried to strangle her. She could feel his breath moving down towards her. She screamed, and she pushed him away, and she suddenly felt like that girl that she’d been a year ago. She felt tears on her cheeks, but she felt her hand grab a rock beside her, and she thrashed it against his head, and she took a deep breath. Her breath was rapid, and she quickly backed away, like that guy had burned her by touching her. It really looked like him. It was his face, and she saw his clothes, his hands, and his hair. Her breath was rapid, and she was taking quick breaths, but she still could not breathe. It felt like she was having an asthma attack, and her lungs grabbed for air, and she looked towards the man. He had fallen unconscious. And she looked towards him, and her knees were curled into her chest, with her arms wrapped around them. She felt so small, and her breath was still panting, like her lungs were made of plastic. But then, she looked towards him, and it was still his face. It was still his body. And so, she slowly crept forward, and she carefully reached down, and she lifted his shut eyelid: it was his eyes. Those eyes that were burned into her memory for eternity. And so… she hit him. Her fist curled, and she struck his cheek. And then she sat atop him, and she hit him again, and there were tears that quietly rolled down her cheeks. She looked down in a small breath, but then she struck him again, and again, and her knuckles started to bleed. She hit him again, and again, and again, and-

    “Kate! Stop.” Someone ordered behind her, and she felt arms wrapping around her waist and pulling her off of the man, and she yelped, and she punched against the arm. “Get off of me!” Kate yelped, and she fought against the arms, but they were stronger than her, and she tried to run again. “Don’t touch me!” She cried, and she tried to fight him, and she tried to get up, and she tried to do anything that she had learned in all of that training. All of that training to make sure no one ever hurt her again… and she was just the same stupid little kid that she had been a year ago. They wrapped their arms around her waist again, and pulled her back, and then one of the hands framed her face, and pulled her to look at Clint’s face, and she stopped, and she saw him looking up towards him with a scared expression. He was kneeling beside her, and one arm was around her waist, and the other one framed her face, and forced her to look at him. He had a few new bruises on his face, and if she could think straight, she’d assume they came for Yelena.

    “Hey, it’s me, okay?” He asked in a quiet voice, and she looked towards him, her tears spilling down her cheeks, and she was frozen, and she went to look back towards the man. Then something unprecedented happened. She felt Clint’s arms holding her back from the man, his face now bloody and beaten, and he hugged her tightly. His arms wrapped around her. “It’s just me. You’re safe with me.” She looked back towards the man, but her eyes narrowed, and then she realized that that guy was wearing a red tracksuit, and a gold chain. He had a sleeve of tattoos, and he was bald… and he wasn’t the man. It wasn’t real. None of it was. It was some Tracksuits, and she almost had just beat him to death. Her knuckles were bloodied as all hell. She was quiet, and she felt Clint hugging her tightly, but her eyes settled on that man, his eyes bruised dark and his nose bloody… from her. She had a panic attack. … me? She thought, and she looked down, and her wrists stung like hell. It was quiet, but her eyes were still scanning every shadow around them, and every bush, like the next guy that came out really would be him. She didn’t even think to hug him back, she was just… paralyzed. Whatever thought that was in her head was gone. All she knew was that the air was too thick to breathe, and it felt like it burned, and there was this chill in her back and this lump in her throat that wouldn’t leave. It was all terrifying, and foriegn, and new… even if he was there or not. Her breathing only calmed down for maybe a moment, and she felt Clint loosen his arms, and he looked towards her, but she was watching the trees. She was looking for that guy. She was sure that there was something hiding in those shadows. “Hey.”

    She didn’t even hear him, and he nudged her, and after a few moments, she looked towards him. “Hey. Take a breath, okay?” He asked in a slow voice, but she was so overwhelmed. He had never seen her so terrified. He crashed the truck into the side of the road to get Yelena off of her, but he made it so much worse. He knew every corner of the city and still, he didn’t realize that they were about to drive into Central Park. And worse, she’d gone farther than he did. She was completely and utterly alone, and he was so preoccupied with fighting Yelena that it took him a few seconds to realize that they were in Central Park, and Kate was alone. He’d just knocked Yelena out as quickly as he could, and he’d been calling her name through the park. Evidently, she did not hear him, because she had been nearly beating a man to death because she thought that it was that guy. He didn’t realize that he cared about her until he was stuck for three minutes, sweeping through the park to look for her. He had not been that scared since the day that his family was snapped away. He wished that he didn’t drive them into Central Park.

    She sort of heard him speaking, but her eyes were on that guy that she’d beaten. It looked so much like him. Was it really not him? How wasn’t it him? “Take a breath for me, just a deep breath.” He asked, and he reached up, and he held the side of her face with his hand again gently, and he made her look towards him. “Okay? In and out.” Clint nodded, and she took a shaky breath, but it was better than the hyperventilating inhales that she was struggling to even do a moment ago. “Okay?” He asked, but she barely nodded, and he looked around them, and he stood, and he reached down and laced his fingers around hers. She looked back towards that man, and all of the blood on his face, and the blood that was running down her knuckles. “Come on.” He nodded, and he looked down towards her, and she slowly looked up towards him, and after a few moments she stood. Her eyes looked around them, like her head was on a swivel, and she watched every single tree. She watched every damn blade of grass, and every shadow. “ Katie. ” He called again, and he looked at her with a deeply concerned voice, and she slowly looked back towards him. “Come on.” He nodded, and he squeezed her hand, and it took a few moments, but she slowly followed behind him, and she looked back towards that bloodied guy on the grass.

    She still had tears that rolled down her cheeks, but she didn’t make a sound: she was so quiet, but she looked absolutely terrified. Her body language was so tight and constricted, and she probably didn’t realize it, but her hand was holding onto his like it was her only tether to life, like he was her only tether to life. They had to walk through the park to get out, and he noticed that she lagged behind, but she didn’t let go of his hand. He slowed down so that they walked beside each other, and he kept her close to him. He hadn’t ever seen her so quiet, or so scared, or so observant. Her eyes took in every single branch that snapped, and if they passed by someone that walked by them, she’d look at them until they looked at her after they’d passed with a strange glance. “Hey, you’re safe with me.” Clint assured her again, and he looked back towards her, but she barely even acknowledged him. She just shallowly nodded, and she looked back towards the trees again. Just gotta get back home. He thought, and finally they approached the iron-clad gates of the gate, and she looked up towards them, her eyes still glazed over with terror.

    When they finally got out, Kate looked back, and he paused, letting her have that moment, but after a few moments, he squeezed her hand again, and he started to walk again, and she slowly followed him. She reached up and she wiped her tears from her cheeks, but there were still a few tears that rolled down her cheeks afterward. Clint looked back towards her, but she hadn’t said one word. She just looked to the right, where the park was still visible. It was like this ghost watching over her shoulder, and she cringed. He looked to the left, and he let a few taxi-cabs and cars pass by, and then he nudged her gently, and they walked across the street, a little further from the park. She didn’t say anything, but she looked a little less scared. Her hand hung on the back of her neck, and she looked behind them as they walked back towards the apartment. Every person that passed them could be him , just wearing another mask. “Almost there, Katie.” He nodded, and he looked back towards her, and she was walking less than a foot away from him. Suddenly everything about the city that he loved was cold and foreign and terrifying, like all she saw was the freezing metal of the sky-scrapers. Not the warmth of the lights, and the mix of smells, and the food trucks, and the men with signs and pretzels that snag in each street. All of humanity was gone, and it was only cold metal, and stares that ran down every inch of her skin. But there was one person, and that was Clint: that was the only person besides him , and then all of the cold. So she stayed close to him. And then… were they there? It felt like either ten hours or ten minutes since they had left those gates. He took his key from his pocket, and he reached forward, and he pressed one of the elevator’s buttons to their safe-house. They stepped in the elevator, and she was quiet, and she looked at the doors like they would just come forward and attack her. Her hand was tight on the edges of her sleeves, and she looked towards the doors. Everything seemed claustrophobic and horrible. She hadn’t been back there since that night. She thought maybe she had healed. Maybe she had gotten over it like Jack and her mom wanted her to. Well, this panic attack, this night-long nightmare made it clear that she had not made an inch of progress. “Are you okay?” He asked her, and he looked towards her, and when he said it out loud, he realized that was probably the stupidest damn question in the world. She glanced towards him, and she still looked so scared, and he looked into her eyes. “... sorry.” She mumbled, and she looked down again. It was the first word that she’d said. Her voice sounded like a different person. Her voice was hoarse and slightly broken, and it sounded so un-Kate-like. She was smiles, and giggles, and jokes, and everything that was nice. And she looked so scared now, and he felt completely useless. “... I didn’t realize that guy wasn’t…” She cut her sentence off, and she looked away, her face hot with humiliation, and he looked towards her, but she looked down at her feet. How didn’t she see that? How did she really think that it was him? She felt like she was suddenly going crazy.     “He’ll have a hell of a headache tomorrow, but he’ll be fine, Katie.” Clint assured her, and she nodded, and some of that weight seemed to be taken off. She didn’t realize until now how badly she was screwed up from that night. She fell into the false thought that time just made it all better. It didn’t. It clearly didn’t. The elevator doors dinged, and they slowly opened, and Kate slowly moved forward, her arms crossing tightly across her chest. She looked like she just wanted to disappear into herself. She took her key for the apartment from her pocket, and she slowly slipped it in, and twisted it, and she let the door hang open for Clint. He stepped in behind her, and he shut the door, and he locked it. He heard Clint stepping towards the bedroom down the hall, but he called her name, and after a moment she turned back towards him. She wanted to go hide in that room, but she nodded, and trudged towards the kitchen table. She sat down in one of the chairs, and she looked down towards the wooden table.

    He whistled for Lucky, and he reached down and gave him a slice of pizza from a box that laid on the table. Lucky retreated back toward shis bed with the slice hanging from his teeth. Clint reached forward and took their basket of medical supplies, and he sat down before her. She looked so small. Her converse, and the blood red of the fabric that they had once carried were replaced with the faded color of blood. I just haven’t been there since….” She looked away, and she noticed Lucky looking up towards her with a curious gaze, and she looked away. “... I’m just scrambled up.” She nodded, and she looked back down again, her legs still curled into her chest. Clint reached forward, and he took her hands, and he took one of the alcohol rags.     Clint looked up, and she’d changed into her pajamas, but she walked back down towards them, and she sat beside them, with Lucky in between them. He kissed her hand, and she looked down to the dog with the smallest blink of a smile. But it was there, and it relieved Clint somewhat to see her smile again. Some normal part of her had returned, and it was very welcome.

    “Okay.”

She quickly got up and she slashed the shard back, and it cut across the center of his hand, and he groaned, and held his hand.

    “Way to make it personal, man.” She panted, catching her breath, and she stood before Clint’s unconscious body, protecting him. She caught her breath, and she took another arrow from her quiver, and she waited for him to charge again. She felt her uneven waves of hair barely fall past her shoulders, and she sighed, mourning her long hair for a moment. This guy sucks so much. Kate thought, and she glanced up towards those swords that had been hanging on the wall. They were still attached, but they looked loose. And the glass that had been covering them was broken when Fisk broke through the wall. The purple arrow across Clint’s chest was dusted over with the dust from the debris. He was still out.

    “You’re new.”

    “Old enough to still kick your ass.” Kate shrugged, catching her breath, and she still stood before Clint, waiting for him to wake up, but he protected her that night in Central Park. He got her home safe, and he probably made things a lot less terrifying for her. She owed him, and she wanted to pay it back.

    “You’ve got fire.” Fisk nodded, and she gagged; he was a creep. He went to charge for her again, but she slid underneath in between his legs, and he looked back. His other hand reached for Clint’s body, but her eyes widened, and she quickly took a net arrow from her quiver, and she fired it. The net wrapped around Fisk’s arm, and kept him to the wall so that Clint was out of his reach. God, wake up, Barton. She thought, and she took another putty arrow, and she fired against his other hand, and it stuck against him. He looked down, and he tried to tear his hand from the putty with a small roar. God, he was just like a gorilla, and it was sort of terrifying.

    “Stay away from him.” Kate nodded, and she took a few sparing seconds of peace to catch her breath. Her head dipped for a moment, and she felt her hair tickle her shoulders. She frowned, and pulled her hair behind her ears to get it out of her face. She looked up to Fisk, who was looking at her like a bird that he wanted to strangle. “Your time is over, Fisk. And you need to stop coming after us.” Kate nodded, still catching her breath, but she reached down, and just as she was going to drag Clint’s body further away from Fisk, Fisk’s arm suddenly broke from the net arrow. Her eyes widened, and she quickly recoiled from him, and reached up and took one of the swords that had fallen to the ground. A metallic gash now laid on the center of his bow.

She was getting tired, and fencing wasn’t easy when you were tired. “Why does everyone keep saying that?” Kate groaned, and Fisk slashed at her, but she parried it away, and the swords released a metallic scream. “You’re a very talkative bad guy.” Kate hissed as he slashed towards her once more, and Kate countered, and she dove her sword at him. She took an arrow from Clint’s quiver beside her, and then his bow, just as Clint thrust his sword at her again. It missed and went past Fisk, but then it bounced back, and striked the hind of his head. He suddenly went slack, but she was in front of him, and when he fell, she was caught underneath him. He fell atop her, and Kate’s eyes widened. He was so heavy, and it felt like an avalanche had fallen atop her.

    “ God . ” She breathed, and she coughed, and it was like she couldn’t breathe with this gorilla of a human atop her. But finally, she was able to slip out from underneath him, and she took a deep, clean breath. She caught her breath, but her gaze directed towards Clint. She quickly crawled towards him, slowly standing, and kneeling beside him, and she shook him. “Hey, please have a pulse.” Kate breathed desperately, and she pressed her first two fingers against his neck, and she exhaled when she felt a pretty strong pulse hiding in his body. “Okay.” Kate exhaled, and she let her head hang in relief for a moment. She looked behind them to make sure Fisk was unconscious, and she nodded. She reached up, and felt the waves of her hair fall just above her shoulders. She sighed, and she brushed it out with her fingers, and it was uneven. Not so much, it had been a clean cut, and it was to save her life, but it was still uneven. Her hair was wavy because of how short it was suddenly.

She bit her lip, and she reached down, and she sighed, and she slapped him very gently. “Hey.” She called in a louder voice, and she slapped him again a little harder, enough to make a sound this time, and finally, he came awake with a start, and he sat up quickly. How the hell did Kate manage alone? He was actually, God forbid, impressed. But he slowly took her hand, and she helped him up.

She handed it to lint. “... think this is ours.” Kate exhaled, and he took it, and he picked his quiver and his bow up from the ground. She slowly bent down and took hers. She barely had a scratch on her; that was pretty remarkable. The first time he’d tried to take Fisk when he was Ronin, he came home with three broken ribs and a shattered collar-bone… and Kate was barely an intern super-hero. “... hey, I could really use some coffee right now, what do you think about getting out of here?” She asked with a small breath, and he nodded with a small smile that crawled across his lips. His head hurt like hell. He realized he probably had a concussion. There were a few drops of blood that came from a cut in his head. “Yeah, I think that’s a good idea.” Clint nodded, and she picked up the trick arrows that weren’t shattered, and slipped them back into her quiver. “How the hell did you knock him out?” Clint exhaled, looking back as they began to walk back out towards the original room, and Kate shrugged with a small smile.

She looked at the uneven curls of her hair with a frown. Clint reached up, and he took a grapple arrow from his quiver, and he fired it at the opposite side of the room. He slowly grabbed onto it, and he climbed up and sat on the edge of the window sill. She was exhausted, and he looked pretty tired, too. She missed Lucky suddenly, but she also wanted coffee. So they started to walk back to the apartment.

He groaned, and he sat down against the couch, and he took a box of frozen pizza from the coffee table, and he held it against his head. Kate smiled for a moment: he looked like a damn corpse. It was sort of funny. Kate carefully leaned her quiver against the wall, with Clint’s. She slowly looked up, and she looked at Fisk’s name on the top of her flash-card hierarchy. He didn’t really live up to the name too much. She took the fresh pot of coffee from the kitchen counter, and she poured herself a mug, and took a sip of the black coffee. Clint thought black coffee was disgusting, but she didn’t mind it. He made fun of her for it.

She leaned against it for a moment, regaining her energy for a second. She reached into the closet, and she took her pajamas from a drawer. She slowly slipped the top of her suit over her head, and replaced it with a hoodie that was much more comfortable. There was a purple target across her chest. She then slipped into lavender pajama pants, and she looked back into the mirror, and looked at her short hair. She frowned, and she pursed her lips together. She opened the bedroom door, and she ventured down towards the bathroom a few feet away. She carefully opened the medicine cabinet behind the mirror, and she took a pair of scissors from a shelf in the cabinet. She carefully straightened her hair out with her fingers, and she reached up, and carefully cut the edges, trying to make them all even.

    “Hey, I can help out.” Clint called from behind her, and he leaned against the wall behind her outside the bathroom.

    She turned around, lowering the scissors, and she shot him a look. “Were you a barber in a previous life, Barton?” She asked in a joking voice, and she walked out of the bathroom, down back towards the main-room, and he followed her with a shrug.

    “Well, no, but I have a teenage daughter. I’ve done my fair share of hair-cuts.” Clint responded, and she looked back towards him with a small smile, and he shrugged. “Pay-back for saving my ass.” Clint added, and she smiled, and there was a look of amusement across her features. She looked towards Lucky, and Lucky’s tongue hung from his mouth in what looked like a smile.

    “Okay… sure.” Kate shook her head with a small smile, and he nodded, and he took the scissors. She sat down on the seat before the kitchen’s island, and he stood behind her, and took one of their rags that was usually meant to hold their blood back from fresh wounds, but this was a new use. “Hey, what do you think’s on that drive?” Kate asked, and she felt him pull her hair back on her shoulders gently, and she felt as he carefully pulled a few inches of it together gently, and he cut a little bit of the edge off to make it more even. Clint shrugged, and he brushed another few inches of her hair together with his fingers, and he carefully cut the edges again. She reminded him a lot of Lila, now that he thought about it. He realized that he was really happy that Lila was anything like Kate. He thought that he’d be lucky if Lila grew up to be anything like Kate. She was passionate, and she was smart, and she kicked bad-guy ass, but mostly, she was kind. Kate was a very kind person.

    She didn’t even think to judge Clint about what he did as Ronin… and he did horrible, terrible things. But Kate Bishop did not judge people. She was sort of like Nat in that aspect. Nat would’ve liked her; he knew that. “Hey, maybe we’ll just get a signed video confession on that drive.” Kate suggested, and he nodded with a small, quiet laugh, and Lucky ran up towards her and jumped up with his front two paws on Kate’s lap. Either way, there was a silent agreement that they’d figure it out tomorrow. It was past midnight, and they were both exhausted.

    “Hey, stay still.” Clint warned, and she smiled, and nodded, and stayed still as he continued to even the edges of her hair with the scissors. She looked up to Clint a lot more than she had before they met. She thought that he was so cool before: she thought that there were no flaws in him. He was a perfect superhero. But now she knew that he was just… human. Like everyone else. And that made her look up to him and respect him even more. He was just more of her hero than he had been before. “Okay… all done.” He nodded, and he retreated away with the scissors, and she slowly stood, and pulled a few stray strands of her hair behind her ear again. Lila did that a lot, too.

    “Thanks.” Kate added, and he nodded, and he placed the scissors back down on the kitchen counter. “... okay. I’m checking out.” Kate nodded, and she reached down and pet Lucky, and he gave her hand a kiss before she pulled back, and started to walk down towards the bedroom again.

    “Hey, Kate.”

    “Yeah?” She called back from down the hall, and Clint smiled, and he leaned back against the kitchen counter.

    “Good job tonight.” He added, and he didn’t have to imagine the grin that came across her face, but it still made a small smile grow across his mouth.

    “Night, Clint.”

    “Night, Katie.”

    Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Clint had stayed up a little later that night, with Lucky. Kate was pretty tired, which was fair. She’d gotten in a fist fight with the most powerful man in New York City, and she walked away as the victor. He was impressed. He might even admit, God forbid, that he had underestimated her when they first met. He probably gave her a little too much grief. She was just a kid. She still was. She was twenty, and in his eyes, that was like she was as young as Lila. That was pretty crazy to him. It humbled him, though. He was lucky that he wasn’t a spy until a few good decades into his life. She was barely twenty, and she was already being hunted down by some of the most powerful men in the world. Mostly because of him. It was one day until Christmas. And that meant that that afternoon, Kate would force him to GCT and she would force him to get on a train and go back to his family. He had twenty four hours to make sure that he wouldn’t wake up on Christmas morning to her eulogy. He would never forgive himself if she died, and he really did care about Kate now. She reminded him way too much of Lila. And he did not want to see Kate dead. He had to make sure this was fixed, and he had approximately twenty four hours to do that.

    He took the USB drive, still taped with her mother’s name on the end. He carefully slipped it into her laptop, and he watched as the file popup came on her monitor. He opened the file on the computer, and it was one video. His eyes narrowed, and he looked at Lucky, and scratched behind his ears -it was his favorite spot to be petted- before he then opened the file. It buffered for a moment, and he took a small breath, preparing himself for whatever he was about to see. Fisk was brutal, so he wasn’t exactly picturing anything nice on this secretive, seemingly extremely important USB stick. But soon, the video cleared, and he paused it to take in what he was seeing. It looked like a pretty big pent-house, and there was dust and debris around it. It seemed like this was a security tape, possibly recorded from a corner camera. There seemed to be two silhouettes in the center of the frame, and he leaned forward, and narrowed his eyes, but the footage was too fuzzy at first for him to recognize anyone. He slowly pressed play on the tape again, and it was soundless. It looked pretty old, and the technology looked pretty old, too.

    Clint looked to Lucky beside him, and then back to the laptop. He was quiet. He didn’t want to wake Kate up. She earned a good night of sleep. She had saved his life at least twice in less than two hours. That was pretty good for a kindergartener. What is this? He thought, and he wondered why Fisk had fought so hard to protect this. It was seemingly nothing. He pressed play once more, and he watched as the figures started to move. A piece of the wall seemed to be shot off beside them, and he furrowed his eyebrows together, but then his eyes caught on the corner of the screen. There was suddenly a firework that went through his brain, and he read the time-frame on the security camera again. September… 2012. He read, and the text was almost illegible, but only almost. It definitely said 2012, and he looked back to the figures with a curious eye. Once again, he pressed play, and he realized that the interaction did not look friendly between them. One of the figures seemed to be more feminine, while one was certainly that of a pretty well-built man. But then, there seemed to be something long and dark in the woman’s hand, and he watched as whatever it was seemingly went right through the man. His eyes widened, and he pressed the arrow on the keyboard, and rewinded the video. The hell was that?

    He slowly watched again, and saw the masculine figure fall to the ground right after the woman injected him with whatever that dark stick was. It remained in his chest as he fell to the ground. His eyes widened for a moment as he realized what it was. I’m watching someone die right now. He thought, and he rewinded it again, and he watched it one more time. He looked around at the nice furniture, and the chandeliers that were visible in the frame. Whoever these people were, they were rich. Really rich. Having anything more than a closet in the East village was at least five grand a month. You had to be a billionaire to have more than one bedroom. … wait a minute. He thought, and he looked towards Lucky beside him, staring at him with his tongue hanging from his mouth in a smile. He paused for a moment to pet the top of his head, but then he turned back to Kate’s laptop. He opened the files on the computer in a new window, and he went to the pictures. She had most of her pictures on her laptop. He began to scroll through it, hoping that he was wrong. There was a small smile that tugged on his lips as he saw the plethora of selfies that she had with Lucky, who always had that same smile-looking face. She really was just a kid. It was sort of scary. How did a twelve year old get wrapped up in all of this, he thought. Because of his past mistakes. Well, they were almost done. He realized that when he finally got home to his family, he was going to actually miss her, which would’ve seemed very weird at the start of the week. He also felt sort of uneasy about leaving her alone. She had Lucky, but it was Christmas. He sort of had just come into her life for a week, made sure that her entire family fell apart, and then he would be leaving just in time to make sure that she no longer had anyone to spend Christmas with, because her parents were supervillains. He felt pretty horrible. But he also knew she would never come back with him for Christmas because she had just as much of an ego as he did, and he was just as stubborn as her. He sighed, putting the thoughts away, and he scrolled further and further back in her photos. She had a lot on here; everything from at least a few years back. And every now and then there would be some old pictures, with a little girl in them. He realized that little girl was Kate, probably before her father died, and that night in Central Park.

    She looked so happy when she smiled in those photos… so care-free. She looked like she was acting her age. She was a smiling little girl, who still had her childhood. It wasn’t that she didn’t look happy now,, but there was a slight -familiar- weight on her shoulders from everything she’d seen. He had the same weight: every adult did. It was sort of nice to see her look so carefree. Clint thought that she’d looked like Lila when she was younger. But those photos were important, because he had a theory pertaining to that video. In all of the pictures with her as a little girl with her dad or her mom, he looked at the background of the photos. Some of them were outside, and he could recognize the backgrounds as places near the East village. But the inside ones were almost always one place. It looked like a pretty nice pent- house, with a chandelier that dragged down towards the floor. He minimized that tab, and then looked at the chandelier in the video again. He took a good look at it, and then he looked back at one of the photos with a younger Kate and her father, and that exact same chandelier in the background.

He has dirt on all of his ‘co-workers’ in an unlucky case of a civil war. Clint thought, and he shut his eyes for a moment. He slowly realized that there was no way that he was letting Kate watch that video, because it was definitely, unequivocally, one hundred percent Kate’s mother killing her father… like she’d guessed after they found her father’s autopsy photo. A stab wound right through the chest, with shards of the sword’s blade still embedded in his cold, pale flesh. Poor kid. He thought, and he sighed, and hid his fingers in Lucky’s fur, and began to stress-pet him. I have to tell her. He thought, and it took a few moments of him boring his eyes into the laptop, but his feet slowly sat flat on the rug. He stood, and he sighed, but he quietly stepped down the hallway. He raised his hand into a fist, and he went to knock on the half-open bedroom door, but the light of the hallway illuminated her sleeping figure in the bed, hidden beneath the warmth of the blankets. Clint stopped, and he frowned with a guilty expression over his features. She was such a kid. And she looked so peaceful right now. She must’ve been dreaming something good, for once, because there was the smallest tug of an upwards curl to her lips. She had an arrow necklace that hung down across her chest. It reminded him of the one that Natasha had kept. She still had wrapping across her knuckles that she’d forgotten to take off after fighting Fisk. She looked so peaceful. That was what got him. She just looked like a kid: she looked like Lila, sleeping in her bed. His hand slowly fell away from the door and back at his side, and he sighed, rubbing his temples with a quiet exhale. And he slowly stepped away from the bedroom door, and he gently took the door-knob, and closed the bedroom door quietly.

She’d pretended to be completely fine that entire night, but he was a spy; he was meant to see past people’s masks. She was really scared that entire night, and everytime that she thought he wasn’t looking, her eyes would glance towards the windows, or the fire escape, or the doors before they’d return to the television. She was pretty paranoid that entire night, but he understood: you always thought that you were indestructible. You would see bad things on the news, or you would see bad things happen to your friends or family, but you never thought something bad could happen to you. And then something really bad happened, and you were so used to that feeling of invincibility, and it made that sudden terror and trauma and fear in you ten times worse. He’d had that, and he knew she did, too. She was so much like him. But he wanted her to be better… and she was. That was clear by how she took down Fisk by herself. Give her just one more hour before she knows… half an hour… five minutes… just a few seconds before she knows that her mother drove a sword through her father’s heart. He thought over and over, but as he was walking home, he walked at a slower pace. And Steve, and Tony; he had to watch the life dim out of Wanda’s eyes as she realized that Vision was dead, and he watched the dust blow across the wind as his daughter was taken away from him, along with the rest of his family. He had seen a lot of people that he cared about die. And he was not going to let that happen to Kate, because to put it shortly, it sucked… a lot. Grief was like a knife going through you, but when you physically saw that person die, it was just salt on the wound.

    “Yeah.” Kate promised him, and she released his hand, and went back towards the kitchen with the dirty dishes. She exhaled, and she slowly shook it off. It was still nice not being alone on Christmas.

She didn’t think of everything that came with that title: mostly attention. That was why the public figured out who she was in less than a week and a half. He reached back out the window, and he made sure there were no pedestrians before tossing it out of the shattered window again.

    "Hey. Old fashioned movie binge, Hawkeye Junior?" He asked, knowing she probably wouldn't be able to sleep again. He figured out that when things got pretty bad, if she was really badly reminded of that night in Central Park, she usually didn't sleep. Not because she didn't physically contain the ability to sleep at that time, but because of the nightmares. He knew about the nightmares. He had them bad during those five years after Thanos. They were scary: it was like this sledge-hammer of grief and loss and pain going through you over and over, except for her, it was nothing but a cold sky, and pain, and fear. Terror. So she didn't sleep after she was heavily reminded of that night. And he understood. He wished sometimes he hadn't when he had nightmares, so if she didn't sleep, he usually stayed up with her. And they just watched movies. Lila and him did that all the time. Kate liked it. He always watched such old movies, and she thought they were cool, and 'retro,' and he teased her for calling them that.

    "Yeah. Okay, Hawkeye."

    “Bad luck to tell you.” She shrugged, and he smiled, and nodded.
